<td>
<ul><li>2012: As the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Canada" title="Prime Minister of Canada">prime minister of Canada</a> and as a member of Parliament, Harper was awarded the medal as a member of the <a href="/wiki/Canadian_order_of_precedence" title="Canadian order of precedence">Canadian order of precedence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:ACM_ribbon.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/ACM_ribbon.png/100px-ACM_ribbon.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="28" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e3/ACM_ribbon.png 1.5x" data-file-width="106" data-file-height="30" /></a></span></td>
<td><a href="/wiki/Alberta_Centennial_Medal" title="Alberta Centennial Medal">Alberta Centennial Medal</a></td>
<td>
<ul><li>2005</li>
<li>As a prominent Albertan, as a member of Parliament and in particular, leader of the Official Opposition, Harper was awarded with the Alberta Centennial Medal.<sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Harper received the <a href="/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson_Award" class="mw-redirect" title="Woodrow Wilson Award">Woodrow Wilson Award</a> on October 6, 2006, for his public service in Calgary. The awards ceremony was held at the <a href="/wiki/Telus_Convention_Centre" title="Telus Convention Centre">Telus Convention Centre</a> in Calgary, the same place where he made his victory speech.<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Time_(magazine)" title="Time (magazine)"><i>Time</i> magazine</a> also named him as <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Newsmaker_of_the_Year_(Time)" title="Canadian Newsmaker of the Year (Time)">Canada's Newsmaker of the Year</a> in 2006. Stephen Handelman wrote "that the prime minister who was once dismissed as a doctrinaire backroom tactician with no experience in government has emerged as a warrior in power".<sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On June 27, 2008, Harper was awarded the Presidential Gold Medallion for Humanitarianism by <a href="/wiki/B%27nai_B%27rith" title="B&#39;nai B&#39;rith">B'nai B'rith</a> International. He is the first Canadian to be awarded this medal.<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On July 11, 2011, Harper was honoured by Alberta's <a href="/wiki/Kainai_Nation" title="Kainai Nation">Blood tribe</a>. He was made Honorary Chief of the Kainai Nation during a ceremony, in which they recognized him for making an <a href="/wiki/Indian_Residential_Schools_Truth_and_Reconciliation_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian Residential Schools Truth and Reconciliation Commission">official apology on behalf of the Government of Canada</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Indian_residential_school_system" title="Canadian Indian residential school system">residential schools abuse</a>. Harper issued this apology in 2008. The chief of the tribe explained that he believes the apology officially started the healing and rebuilding of relations between the federal and native councils. <a href="/wiki/Lester_B._Pearson" title="Lester B. Pearson">Lester B. Pearson</a>, John Diefenbaker, and Jean Chrétien are the only other prime ministers of Canada to have been awarded the same honorary title.<sup id="cite_ref-281" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-281">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On September 27, 2012, Harper received the World Statesman of the Year award. This award was offered through a US group of various faith representatives. This occurred at a black tie banquet in New York. Jean Chrétien was one of the previous recipients from Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-282" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-282">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In August 2016 <a href="/wiki/President_of_Ukraine" title="President of Ukraine">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko" title="Petro Poroshenko">Petro Poroshenko</a> of <a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine">Ukraine</a> awarded Stephen Harper with the highest award for foreigners–the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Liberty_(Ukraine)" title="Order of Liberty (Ukraine)">Order of Liberty</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-283" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-283">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In December 2019, it was announced by Governor General <a href="/wiki/Julie_Payette" title="Julie Payette">Julie Payette</a> that Harper had been appointed as a Companion of the Order of Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-284" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-284">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-285" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-285">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> He was formally <a href="/wiki/Investiture" title="Investiture">invested</a> on September 18, 2022, by Governor General <a href="/wiki/Mary_Simon" title="Mary Simon">Mary Simon</a> in <a href="/wiki/London" title="London">London</a> in a ceremony attended by incumbent Prime Minister Justin Trudeau, and former prime ministers <a href="/wiki/Kim_Campbell" title="Kim Campbell">Kim Campbell</a>, Jean Chrétien, Paul Martin and former Governors-General Michaëlle Jean and <a href="/wiki/David_Johnston" title="David Johnston">David Johnston</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2023, he was appointed a Member of the <a href="/wiki/Alberta_Order_of_Excellence" title="Alberta Order of Excellence">Alberta Order of Excellence</a>, the province's civilian honour for merit.<sup id="cite_ref-AOE_274-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AOE-274">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg/220px-Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg/330px-Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg/440px-Stephen_Harper_%26_Larry_Smith_watched_football_game.jpg 2x" data-file-width="984" data-file-height="619" /></a><figcaption>Stephen Harper and former <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Football_League" title="Canadian Football League">CFL</a> player <a href="/wiki/Larry_Smith_(Canadian_politician)" title="Larry Smith (Canadian politician)">Larry Smith</a> watching a 2012 football game in <a href="/wiki/Montreal" title="Montreal">Montreal</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg/220px-Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg/330px-Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg/440px-Harper_Canada_Day_09.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2464" data-file-height="1632" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Stephen Harper speaking at 2009 <a href="/wiki/Canada_Day" title="Canada Day">Canada Day</a> celebrations on <a href="/wiki/Parliament_Hill" title="Parliament Hill">Parliament Hill</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ottawa" title="Ottawa">Ottawa</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Harper married <a href="/wiki/Laureen_Harper" title="Laureen Harper">Laureen Teskey</a> on December 11, 1993.<sup id="cite_ref-289" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-289">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup> Laureen was formerly married to <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealander</a> Neil Fenton from 1985 to 1988.<sup id="cite_ref-kingston_290-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kingston-290">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> The Harpers have two children: Benjamin and Rachel. Harper is the third prime minister, after Pierre Trudeau and <a href="/wiki/John_Turner" title="John Turner">John Turner</a>, to send his children to <a href="/wiki/Rockcliffe_Park_Public_School" title="Rockcliffe Park Public School">Rockcliffe Park Public School</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Ottawa" title="Ottawa">Ottawa</a>.
</p><p>In the late 1980s, he became an <a href="/wiki/Evangelical" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical">evangelical Christian</a>, a member of RockPointe Church (<a href="/wiki/Christian_and_Missionary_Alliance" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian and Missionary Alliance">Christian and Missionary Alliance</a>) in Bearspaw, a suburb of <a href="/wiki/Calgary" title="Calgary">Calgary</a>, Alberta.<sup id="cite_ref-291" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-291">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> After moving to Ottawa in 2003, he became a member of East Gate Alliance Church.<sup id="cite_ref-292" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-292">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An avid follower of <a href="/wiki/Ice_hockey" title="Ice hockey">ice hockey</a>, he has been a fan of the <a href="/wiki/Toronto_Maple_Leafs" title="Toronto Maple Leafs">Toronto Maple Leafs</a> since his childhood in the Leaside and Etobicoke communities in Toronto.<sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup> Harper is also a fan of the <a href="/wiki/Calgary_Flames" title="Calgary Flames">Calgary Flames</a>. He published a book, <i><a href="/wiki/A_Great_Game:_The_Forgotten_Leafs_and_the_Rise_of_Professional_Hockey" class="mw-redirect" title="A Great Game: The Forgotten Leafs and the Rise of Professional Hockey">A Great Game: The Forgotten Leafs and the Rise of Professional Hockey</a></i> (2013), which chronicles the growth of professional hockey, particularly in Toronto,<sup id="cite_ref-294" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-294">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup> and writes articles occasionally on the subject.<sup id="cite_ref-295" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-295">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup> Harper appeared on <a href="/wiki/The_Sports_Network" title="The Sports Network">The Sports Network</a> (TSN) during the broadcast of the Canada–Russia final of the <a href="/wiki/2007_World_Junior_Ice_Hockey_Championships" title="2007 World Junior Ice Hockey Championships">2007 World Junior Ice Hockey Championships</a>. He was interviewed and expressed his views on the state of hockey and his preference for an overtime period in lieu of a shoot-out.<sup id="cite_ref-296" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-296">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup> In February 2010, Harper interviewed former <a href="/wiki/National_Hockey_League" title="National Hockey League">National Hockey League</a> greats <a href="/wiki/Wayne_Gretzky" title="Wayne Gretzky">Wayne Gretzky</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gordie_Howe" title="Gordie Howe">Gordie Howe</a> for a Saskatoon Kinsmen Club charity event.<sup id="cite_ref-297" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-297">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Harper had a <a href="/wiki/Cameo_appearance" title="Cameo appearance">cameo appearance</a> in an episode of the television show <i><a href="/wiki/Corner_Gas" title="Corner Gas">Corner Gas</a>,</i> which aired March 12, 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-298" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-298">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup> He owns an extensive <a href="/wiki/Gramophone_record" class="mw-redirect" title="Gramophone record">vinyl record</a> collection and is a fan of <a href="/wiki/The_Beatles" title="The Beatles">The Beatles</a> and <a href="/wiki/AC/DC" title="AC/DC">AC/DC</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-299" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-299">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup> In October 2009, he joined <a href="/wiki/Yo-Yo_Ma" title="Yo-Yo Ma">Yo-Yo Ma</a> on stage in a <a href="/wiki/National_Arts_Centre" title="National Arts Centre">National Arts Centre</a> gala and performed "<a href="/wiki/With_a_Little_Help_from_My_Friends" title="With a Little Help from My Friends">With a Little Help from My Friends</a>". He was also accompanied by Herringbone, an Ottawa band with whom he regularly practices.<sup id="cite_ref-300" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-300">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> He received a standing ovation after providing the piano accompaniment and lead vocals for the song.<sup id="cite_ref-301" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-301">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2010, Harper taped a cameo appearance in an episode of the television show <i><a href="/wiki/Murdoch_Mysteries" title="Murdoch Mysteries">Murdoch Mysteries</a></i>, which aired July 20, 2011, during the show's fourth season.<sup id="cite_ref-CityNews20101015_302-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CityNews20101015-302">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-globeandmailcameo_303-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-globeandmailcameo-303">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was the first prime minister to employ a personal stylist, Michelle Muntean, whose duties range from coordinating his clothing to preparing his hair and makeup for speeches and television appearances. While she used to be on the public payroll, she has been paid for by the Conservative Party since "some time [in] 2007".<sup id="cite_ref-304" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-304">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2013, the Harper family had two cats, Stanley and Gypsy.<sup id="cite_ref-305" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-305">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup> They have fostered other cats as well.<sup id="cite_ref-306" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-306">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
